## Implementation

I need to:
1. Precompute Fibonacci numbers up to 10^5
2. For each N, compute the sum of F(i) × F(N-2-i) for i from 0 to N-2
3. Return result modulo 10^9+7

```python
def solve():
    MOD = 1000000007
    
    # Precompute Fibonacci numbers up to max possible N (10^5)
    max_n = 100000
    fib = [0] * (max_n + 2)
    fib[0] = 1
    if max_n >= 1:
        fib[1] = 2
    
    for i in range(2, max_n + 1):
        fib[i] = (fib[i-1] + fib[i-2]) % MOD
    
    T = int(input())
    for _ in range(T):
        N = int(input())
        
        if N == 1:
            # For N=1, no consecutive pairs possible, so counter is 0
            # ZeroShark returns true only if counter == 1, which never happens
            print(0)
        else:
            # Sum over i from 0 to N-2 of fib[i] * fib[N-2-i]
            result = 0
            for i in range(N - 1):  # i from 0 to N-2
                result = (result + fib[i] * fib[N - 2 - i]) % MOD
            print(result)

solve()
```

```python
fib = [0] * (max_n + 2)
fib[0] = 1
if max_n >= 1:
    fib[1] = 2

for i in range(2, max_n + 1):
    fib[i] = (fib[i-1] + fib[i-2]) % MOD

T = int(input())
for _ in range(T):
    N = int(input())
    
    if N == 1:
        print(0)
    else:
        result = 0
        # Sum over i from 0 to N-2 of fib[i] * fib[N-2-i]
        for i in range(N - 1):
            result = (result + fib[i] * fib[N - 2 - i]) % MOD
        print(result)
```

You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

A binary string of length N is a string of N characters, where each character is either 0 or 1. 

Wet Shark makes a list binStrings, consisting of all 2^N N-digit binary strings. For each string X in binStrings, Wet Shark runs a function ZeroShark(X), defined as follows (psuedocode):

bool ZeroShark(x):
//note the zero indexing
n = length(x)
counter = 0
FOR i = 1 to n-1
    IF x[i] == '0' and x[i - 1] == '0'
        INCREMENT counter
        END IF
ENDFOR
return (counter == 1)

Wet Shark stores an element X in a new list, binTrues  if ZeroShark(X) returns True for that X . In otherwords, binTrues consists of all the elements X in binStrings such that ZeroShark(X) returns True.

Given T testcases, where each testcase consists of integer N, calculate the length of binTrues for each N. Because the lists can get large, output your answer modulo 10^9+7=1000000007

INPUT

The first line of the input contains a single integer T, the number of such N.

The next T lines of the input contain a single integer N, as described in the problem.

OUTPUT

Output T separate lines, each containing the number of elements in the resulting list binTrues for each N in the input. The lists may be large, so output the answer modulo 10^9+7=1000000007

CONSTRAINTS

1 ≤ T ≤ 10

1 ≤ N ≤ 10^5

SAMPLE INPUT
2

2

3

SAMPLE OUTPUT
1

2

Calling ZeroShark on each element in binStrings, the function only returns true when X is equal to 001 or 100. 

Therefore, binTrues is equal to ['001', '100'], and has a length of 2.

```python
def solve(n):
    if n == 1:
        return 0
    if n == 2:
        return 1
    # For n >= 3
    # result = 2^(n-2) + (n-3) * 2^(n-4)
    power = pow(2, n-4, 1000000007)
    return (pow(2, n-2, 1000000007) + (n-3) * power) % 1000000007
```

But note that in modular arithmetic, for negative exponents, we use modular inverse.

For negative exponent: 2^(-k) mod m = (2^k)^(-1) mod m

For n=3: we want (3-3) * 2^(-1) = 0 * (2^(-1)) = 0

So if we define 2^(-1) mod 1000000007, it exists.

For any k >= 0, 2^(-k) mod m = (2^k)^(-1) mod m

In Python, pow(2, k, m) computes 2^k mod m, and pow(2, -k, m) computes the modular inverse.

But in our case, for n=3: k = n-4 = -1, so we want 2^(-1) mod m.

We can compute pow(2, n-4, 1000000007) when n-4 >= 0, and when n-4 < 0, we can compute it as the modular inverse.

But since (n-3) is 0 when n=3, it doesn't matter.

For n=4: n-4=0, 2^0=1
For n=5: n-4=1, 2^1=2

So in code, we can write:

```python
mod = 1000000007
if n == 1:
    return 0
if n == 2:
    return 1
# n >= 3
term1 = pow(2, n-2, mod)
term2 = 0
if n > 3:
    term2 = (n-3) * pow(2, n-4, mod) % mod
return (term1 + term2) % mod
```

For n=3: term2 = 0, so result = 2^(1) = 2 ✓
For n=4: 2^2 + 1 * 2^0 = 4 + 1 = 5 ✓

```python
            term2 = (N-3) * pow(2, N-4, mod) % mod
        result = (term1 + term2) % mod
        print(result)
```

Problem

You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

One day Vasya was on a physics practical, performing the task on measuring the capacitance. He followed the teacher's advice and did as much as n measurements, and recorded the results in the notebook. After that he was about to show the results to the teacher, but he remembered that at the last lesson, the teacher had made his friend Petya redo the experiment because the largest and the smallest results differed by more than two times. Vasya is lazy, and he does not want to redo the experiment. He wants to do the task and go home play computer games. So he decided to cheat: before Vasya shows the measurements to the teacher, he will erase some of them, so as to make the largest and the smallest results of the remaining measurements differ in no more than two times. In other words, if the remaining measurements have the smallest result x, and the largest result y, then the inequality y ≤ 2·x must fulfill. Of course, to avoid the teacher's suspicion, Vasya wants to remove as few measurement results as possible from his notes.

Help Vasya, find what minimum number of measurement results he will have to erase from his notes so that the largest and the smallest of the remaining results of the measurements differed in no more than two times.


-----Input-----

The first line contains integer n (2 ≤ n ≤ 10^5) — the number of measurements Vasya made. The second line contains n integers c_1, c_2, ..., c_{n} (1 ≤ c_{i} ≤ 5000) — the results of the measurements. The numbers on the second line are separated by single spaces.


-----Output-----

Print a single integer — the minimum number of results Vasya will have to remove.


-----Examples-----
Input
6
4 5 3 8 3 7

Output
2

Input
4
4 3 2 4

Output
0



-----Note-----

In the first sample you can remove the fourth and the sixth measurement results (values 8 and 7). Then the maximum of the remaining values will be 5, and the minimum one will be 3. Or else, you can remove the third and fifth results (both equal 3). After that the largest remaining result will be 8, and the smallest one will be 4.

The input will be stdin and you should print your solution to stdout

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have n measurements with values c₁, c₂, ..., cₙ
2. We need to remove the minimum number of measurements so that the largest remaining value ≤ 2 × smallest remaining value
3. We want to minimize the number of removals (maximize the number of remaining measurements)

## Key Insights

1. If we sort the measurements, we can consider all possible subarrays where the maximum ≤ 2 × minimum
2. For any subarray (contiguous in sorted order), if max ≤ 2 × min, then it's a valid solution
3. The number of removals = n - (length of the longest valid subarray)
4. We need to find the longest subarray where the last element ≤ 2 × first element

## Algorithm Steps

1. Sort the array of measurements
2. Use a two-pointer (sliding window) approach to find the longest subarray where the maximum ≤ 2 × minimum
3. For each possible starting position, extend the window as far as possible while maintaining the condition
4. Track the maximum length of such valid subarrays
5. Answer = n - maximum_length

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Sort the array** - this allows us to easily find contiguous subarrays
2. **Use two pointers** - left pointer starts at 0, right pointer moves forward
3. **For each left position**, move right pointer while `arr[right] ≤ 2 * arr[left]`
4. **Track the maximum window size** found
5. **Return n - max_window_size**

## Edge Cases

- If all values are already within the 2x ratio, we need 0 removals
- If values are too dispersed, we might need to remove many elements

Let me trace through the examples:

**Example 1**: [4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7]
- Sorted: [3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]
- Starting at 0: 3,3,4,5 → 5 ≤ 2×3 = 6 ✓ (length 4)
- Starting at 1: 3,4,5,7 → 7 ≤ 2×3 = 6 ✗
- Starting at 2: 4,5,7 → 7 ≤ 8 ✓ (length 3)
- Starting at 3: 5,7 → 7 ≤ 10 ✓ (length 2)
- Starting at 4: 7 → length 1
- Starting at 5: 8 → length 1
- Maximum length = 4, so removals = 6 - 4 = 2 ✓

**Example 2**: [4, 3, 2, 4]
- Sorted: [2, 3, 4, 4]
- Starting at 0: 2,3,4,4 → 4 ≤ 4 ✓ (length 4)
- Maximum length = 4, so removals = 4 - 4 = 0 ✓

```python
n = int(input())
c = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
c.sort()

max_length = 1  # At minimum, we can always keep one element

# Use two pointers to find the longest valid subarray
left = 0
for right in range(n):
    # While the current element is greater than 2 times the left element,
    # we need to move left pointer forward
    while c[right] > 2 * c[left] and left < right:
        left += 1
    
    # Now c[right] <= 2 * c[left] is satisfied for the current left and right
    # Update the maximum length found so far
    current_length = right - left + 1
    max_length = max(max_length, current_length)

# The answer is the number of measurements to remove
# = total measurements - maximum measurements we can keep
print(n - max_length)
```

    # Update the maximum length found so far
    current_length = right - left + 1
    max_length = max(max_length, current_length)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` measurements with results `c_1, c_2, ..., c_n`
2. We need to remove the minimum number of measurements so that the largest remaining value ≤ 2 × smallest remaining value
3. We want to minimize the number of removals (maximize the number of kept measurements)

## Key Insights

1. If we sort the measurements, we can try different subarrays where the maximum is at most 2 times the minimum
2. For any valid subset, if we sort the values, then `max(subarray) ≤ 2 × min(subarray)`
3. We want to find the longest contiguous subarray (after sorting) that satisfies this condition
4. The answer will be `n - (length of longest valid subarray)`

## Algorithm

1. Sort the measurements
2. For each possible starting position, extend the subarray as far as possible while maintaining `max ≤ 2 × min`
3. Track the maximum length of such valid subarray
4. Return `n - max_length`

## Step-by-step Solution

Let me trace through the examples:

**Example 1**: `[4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7]`
- Sorted: `[3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]`
- Check subarrays:
  - `[3, 3]`: 3 ≤ 2×3 ✓ (length 2)
  - `[3, 4]`: 4 ≤ 2×3 ✓ (length 2) 
  - `[3, 4, 5]`: 5 ≤ 2×3 ✓ (length 3)
  - `[3, 4, 5, 7]`: 7 ≤ 2×3? 7 ≤ 6? ✗
  - So maximum valid length is 3
- Answer: 6 - 3 = 3... but expected is 2.

Wait, let me reconsider. Looking at the note:
"Remove the fourth and the sixth measurement results (values 8 and 7). Then the maximum of the remaining values will be 5, and the minimum one will be 3. Or else, you can remove the third and fifth results (both equal 3). After that the largest remaining result will be 8, and the smallest one will be 4."

After removing 8 and 7: we have `[3, 3, 4, 5]` → min=3, max=5, 5 ≤ 2×3 ✓ (5 ≤ 6)
After removing two 3s: we have `[4, 5, 7, 8]` → min=4, max=8, 8 ≤ 2×4 ✓ (8 ≤ 8)

So valid remaining sets: `[3,3,4,5]` (length 4) or `[4,5,7,8]` (length 4)

Maximum valid length is 4, so answer is 6-4 = 2 ✓

## Corrected Algorithm

For each possible minimum value in the sorted array, we can extend to find the farthest maximum such that max ≤ 2 × min.

```python
n = int(input())
c = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
c.sort()

max_kept = 1  # At minimum, we can keep at least one measurement

# Try each possible starting position as the minimum
for i in range(n):
    # For each starting position i, extend as far as possible
    min_val = c[i]
    max_val = c[i]
    
    # Extend the subarray from position i
    for j in range(i, n):
        max_val = c[j]
        if max_val > 2 * min_val:
            break
        max_kept = max(max_kept, j - i + 1)

print(n - max_kept)
```

Wait, this has O(n²) complexity which might be too slow for n=10⁵. Let me optimize.

## Optimized Approach

Since the array is sorted, we can use two pointers:
- For each left pointer, find the rightmost valid right pointer
- Use sliding window technique

```python
n = int(input())
c = list(map(int, input().split()))

c.sort()

max_kept = 1

# Use two pointers to find longest valid subarray
left = 0
for right in range(n):
    # While the current window violates the condition, move left pointer
    while left <= right and c[right] > 2 * c[left]:
        left += 1
    
    # If we have a valid window from left to right
    if left <= right:
        max_kept = max(max_kept, right - left + 1)

print(n - max_kept)
```

Wait, let me verify this logic. The condition should be: we want the maximum ≤ 2 × minimum.

For a window from `left` to `right`, the minimum is `c[left]` and maximum is `c[right]`.

So we need `c[right] ≤ 2 * c[left]`.

But if we increment `left`, we're reducing the minimum, which makes the condition easier to satisfy. This seems backwards.

Actually, for a fixed right pointer, as we move left from right down to 0, the leftmost element's value might increase, making the condition harder.

Let me fix the two-pointer approach:

```python
n = int(input())
c = list(map(int, input().split()))

c.sort()

max_kept = 1

# For each possible left endpoint, find the longest valid segment
for left in range(n):
    # Start with the current left as minimum
    min_val = c[left]
    right = left
    
    # Extend right as far as possible
    while right < n and c[right] <= 2 * min_val:
        right += 1
    
    # The valid length is from left to right-1
    max_kept = max(max_kept, right - left)

print(n - max_kept)
```

This is O(n²) in worst case. For n=10⁵, this might be too slow.

Let me use a more efficient approach with two pointers:

```python
n = int(input())
c = list(map(int, input().split()))

c.sort()

max_kept = 1
left = 0

# For each right pointer, find the leftmost valid left pointer
for right in range(n):
    # We need c[right] <= 2 * c[left]
    # If c[right] > 2 * c[left], we need to increase left
    while left <= right and c[right] > 2 * c[left]:
        left += 1
    
    # After adjusting left, if left <= right, we have a valid segment
    if left <= right:
        max_kept = max(max_kept, right - left + 1)

print(n - max_kept)
```

This should work: for each right position, we maintain the leftmost position such that c[right] ≤ 2 * c[left]. As right increases, c[right] increases, so the condition might break, and we need to move left right to satisfy it.

```python
n = int(input())
c = list(map(int, input().split()))

c.sort()

max_kept = 1
left = 0

for right in range(n):
    # Move left pointer until the condition is satisfied
    while left <= right and c[right] > 2 * c[left]:
        left += 1
    
    # If we have a valid window from left to right
    if left <= right:
        max_kept = max(max_kept, right - left + 1)

print(n - max_kept)
```

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` measurements with results `c_1, c_2, ..., c_n`
2. We need to remove the minimum number of measurements so that the remaining measurements have the property: `max(remaining) ≤ 2 * min(remaining)`
3. We want to minimize the number of removals (maximize the number of remaining measurements)

## Key Insights

1. After removing some measurements, we want the ratio between max and min to be at most 2
2. Since we want to minimize removals, we should try to keep as many measurements as possible
3. The remaining measurements will form a contiguous subsequence when sorted
4. We need to find the longest contiguous subsequence in the sorted array where `max ≤ 2 * min`

## Solution Approach

1. Sort the measurements
2. Use two pointers (left and right) to find the longest contiguous subsequence where the maximum element is at most twice the minimum element
3. The answer will be `n - (length of longest valid subsequence)`

## Step-by-step Algorithm

1. Sort the array of measurements
2. Initialize two pointers: `left = 0`, `right = 0`
3. Use a sliding window approach:
   - Expand the right pointer while `arr[right] <= 2 * arr[left]`
   - When the condition is violated, move the left pointer until the condition is satisfied again
4. Track the maximum length of valid subsequence
5. Return `n - max_length`

## Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
measurements.sort()

max_remaining = 0

# Use sliding window to find longest subsequence where max <= 2 * min
left = 0
for right in range(n):
    # Shrink window from left while condition is violated
    while measurements[right] > 2 * measurements[left]:
        left += 1
    
    # Now the window [left, right] satisfies the condition
    current_length = right - left + 1
    max_remaining = max(max_remaining, current_length)

# Minimum removals = total measurements - maximum remaining measurements
print(n - max_remaining)
```

## Trace through examples:

**Example 1**: `[4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7]`
- After sorting: `[3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]`
- Window analysis:
  - right=0: [3] - valid, length=1
  - right=1: [3,3] - valid, length=2  
  - right=2: [3,3,4] - 4 ≤ 2×3=6, valid, length=3
  - right=3: [3,3,4,5] - 5 ≤ 2×3=6, valid, length=4
  - right=4: [3,3,4,5,7] - 7 > 2×3=6, so move left to 1: [3,4,5,7] - 7 > 2×3=6, move left to 2: [4,5,7] - 7 > 2×4=8? No, 7 ≤ 8, valid, length=4
  - right=5: [4,5,7,8] - 8 > 2×4=8? No, 8 ≤ 8, valid, length=4
- Maximum remaining = 4, so removals = 6-4 = 2 ✓

**Example 2**: `[4, 3, 2, 4]`
- After sorting: `[2, 3, 4, 4]`
- All consecutive subarrays satisfy: max ≤ 2×min (4 ≤ 2×2=4, 4 ≤ 2×3=6, etc.)
- Maximum remaining = 4, so removals = 4-4 = 0 ✓

The solution works correctly for both examples.

    while measurements[right] > 2 * measurements[left]:
        left += 1
    
    # Now the window [left, right] satisfies the condition
    current_length = right - left + 1
    max_remaining = max(max_remaining, current_length)

# Minimum removals = total measurements - maximum remaining measurements
print(n - max_remaining)

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` measurements with results `c_1, c_2, ..., c_n`
2. We need to remove the minimum number of measurements such that the largest remaining value is at most twice the smallest remaining value
3. In other words, if the remaining values have min `x` and max `y`, then `y ≤ 2x`
4. We want to minimize the number of removals (which means maximizing the number of remaining measurements)

## Approach

Since we want to minimize removals, we should try to keep as many measurements as possible while satisfying the constraint `max ≤ 2 * min`.

Key insights:
1. Sort the measurements to easily work with min and max values
2. For any subset of measurements, if we keep a range of values from index `i` to `j` (inclusive), then:
   - The min is `c[i]`
   - The max is `c[j]`
   - We need `c[j] ≤ 2 * c[i]`
3. We want to find the longest contiguous subsequence (in sorted order) that satisfies this condition
4. The answer will be `n - (length of longest valid subsequence)`

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Read input
2. Sort the array of measurements
3. Use two pointers (left and right) to find the longest valid subsequence
4. For each possible left boundary, find the farthest right boundary such that `c[right] ≤ 2 * c[left]`
5. Track the maximum length found
6. Return `n - max_length`

## Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements to work with ordered values
measurements.sort()

max_remaining = 0

# Try each possible starting position
for left in range(n):
    # For each left position, find the rightmost position where constraint is satisfied
    right = left
    while right < n and measurements[right] <= 2 * measurements[left]:
        right += 1
    
    # The valid subsequence is from left to right-1
    length = right - left
    max_remaining = max(max_remaining, length)

# Minimum removals = total measurements - maximum remaining measurements
print(n - max_remaining)
```

## Trace through examples:

**Example 1:** `[4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7]`
- Sorted: `[3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]`
- left=0 (value=3): right goes to 4 (7≤6? No), so right=3 (5≤6? Yes), right=4 (7≤6? No), so right=3. Length=3
- left=1 (value=3): right goes to 4 (7≤6? No), so right=3. Length=2  
- left=2 (value=4): right goes to 5 (8≤8? Yes), right=6 (out of bounds). Length=4
- left=3 (value=5): right goes to 5 (8≤10? Yes), right=6. Length=3
- left=4 (value=7): right goes to 6. Length=1
- left=5 (value=8): right goes to 6. Length=1
- Max length = 4, removals = 6-4 = 2 ✓

**Example 2:** `[4, 3, 2, 4]`
- Sorted: `[2, 3, 4, 4]`
- left=0 (value=2): right goes to 4 (out of bounds). Right=4, length=4
- Max length = 4, removals = 4-4 = 0 ✓

The solution works correctly!

In other words, if the remaining measurements have the smallest result x, and the largest result y, then the inequality y ≤ 2·x must fulfill. Of course, to avoid the teacher's suspicion, Vasya wants to remove as few measurement results as possible from his notes.

Help Vasya, find what minimum number of measurement results he will have to erase from his notes so that the largest and the smallest of the remaining results of the measurements differed in no more than two times.


-----Input-----

The first line contains integer n (2 ≤ n ≤ 10^5) — the number of measurements Vasya made. The second line contains n integers c_1, c_2, ..., c_{n} (1 ≤ c_{i} ≤ 5000) — the results of the measurements. The numbers on the second line are separated by single spaces.

4 3 2 4

Or else, you can remove the third and fifth results (both equal 3). After that the largest remaining result will be 8, and the smallest one will be 4.

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` measurements with results `c_1, c_2, ..., c_n`
2. We need to remove the minimum number of measurements so that the largest remaining value is at most 2 times the smallest remaining value
3. In other words, if we keep values `x` (minimum) and `y` (maximum), we need `y ≤ 2x`

## Key Insights

1. Since we want to minimize removals, we want to maximize the number of measurements we keep
2. The optimal solution will involve keeping a contiguous subsequence of sorted measurements
3. After sorting the array, we can try all possible contiguous subarrays and check if the maximum/minimum condition is satisfied
4. The number of removals will be `n - (length of longest valid contiguous subarray)`

## Algorithm Steps

1. Sort the measurement results
2. For each possible starting position, try to extend the subarray as far as possible while maintaining `max ≤ 2 * min`
3. Keep track of the longest valid subarray
4. Return `n - length_of_longest_valid_subarray`

## Step-by-step Solution

Let me trace through the examples:

**Example 1:** `[4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7]`
- Sorted: `[3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]`
- We need to find longest contiguous subarray where `max ≤ 2 * min`
- Try subarrays:
  - `[3,3]`: max=3, min=3, 3≤2×3 ✓ (length 2)
  - `[3,3,4]`: 4≤2×3 ✓ (length 3)
  - `[3,3,4,5]`: 5≤2×3 ✓ (length 4)
  - `[3,3,4,5,7]`: 7≤2×3? 7≤6? ✗
  - `[4,5,7]`: 7≤2×4? 7≤8 ✓ (length 3)
  - `[5,7,8]`: 8≤2×5? 8≤10 ✓ (length 3)
  - `[3,4,5]`: 5≤2×3? 5≤6 ✓ (length 3)
- Longest valid subarray has length 4, so we remove `6-4=2` measurements

**Example 2:** `[4, 3, 2, 4]`
- Sorted: `[2, 3, 4, 4]`
- Check entire array: max=4, min=2, 4≤2×2? 4≤4 ✓
- Longest valid subarray has length 4, so we remove `4-4=0` measurements

## Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
measurements.sort()

# Find the longest contiguous subarray where max <= 2 * min
max_keep = 0

# Try all possible starting positions
for start in range(n):
    # For each start, extend as far as possible
    min_val = measurements[start]
    max_val = measurements[start]
    
    # Extend the subarray from start position
    for end in range(start, n):
        max_val = measurements[end]
        if max_val > 2 * min_val:
            # Cannot extend further - break inner loop
            break
        max_keep = max(max_keep, end - start + 1)

# Minimum removals is total measurements minus maximum kept
print(n - max_keep)
```

Wait, I need to optimize this. The current O(n²) solution might be too slow for n=10⁵. Let me think of a better approach.

Actually, since we're dealing with sorted arrays, we can use two pointers:

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
measurements.sort()

max_keep = 0

# Use two pointers to find longest valid subarray
left = 0
for right in range(n):
    # Shrink left pointer if the condition is violated
    while measurements[right] > 2 * measurements[left]:
        left += 1
    
    # Now the subarray from left to right is valid
    max_keep = max(max_keep, right - left + 1)

print(n - max_keep)
```

This is more efficient because for each right pointer, we only move the left pointer forward, not reset it. The overall time complexity is O(n).

        left += 1
    
    # Now the subarray from left to right is valid
    max_keep = max(max_keep, right - left + 1)

print(n - max_keep)

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` measurement results
2. We want to remove the minimum number of measurements so that the largest remaining value ≤ 2 × smallest remaining value
3. We need to find the minimum number of removals

## Key Insights

1. Since we want the ratio of max to min to be ≤ 2, we need to find a subsequence where max/minimum ≤ 2
2. If we sort the array, we can use a sliding window approach to find the longest valid subsequence
3. The minimum removals = total measurements - length of longest valid subsequence

## Approach

1. Sort the array of measurements
2. Use two pointers (left and right) to maintain a window where the rightmost element ≤ 2 × leftmost element
3. For each left pointer, move the right pointer as far as possible while maintaining the condition
4. Track the maximum window size across all possible left positions
5. Answer = n - max_window_size

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Sort the measurements
2. Initialize left = 0, max_length = 1
3. For each right position from 1 to n-1:
   - While the current right element > 2 × left element, increment left
   - Update max_length with the current window size (right - left + 1)
4. Return n - max_length

Let me trace through the first example:
- Input: [4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7]
- Sorted: [3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]
- Check windows:
  - [3,3]: 3 ≤ 2×3 ✓, length = 2
  - [3,4]: 4 ≤ 2×3 ✓, length = 2  
  - [3,5]: 5 ≤ 2×3 ✓, length = 3
  - [3,7]: 7 > 2×3=6 ✗, so we need to move left
  - [4,5]: 5 ≤ 2×4=8 ✓, length = 2
  - [4,7]: 7 ≤ 2×4=8 ✓, length = 3
  - [4,8]: 8 > 2×4=8 ✗, 8 > 8 is false, 8 = 8, so it's valid! (8 ≤ 8 ✓) length = 3
  - [5,7]: 7 ≤ 2×5=10 ✓, length = 3
  - [5,8]: 8 ≤ 2×5=10 ✓, length = 3
  - [7,8]: 8 ≤ 2×7=14 ✓, length = 2

Maximum length = 3, so removals = 6 - 3 = 3. 

Wait, this doesn't match the expected output of 2. Let me reconsider.

Actually, when we have [3,5,7,8], the ratio is 8/3 ≈ 2.67 > 2, so that's invalid.

Let me fix the condition: we need max ≤ 2 × min.

For [3,5,7,8]: max=8, min=3, 8 > 2×3=6, so invalid.

For [3,5,7]: 7 ≤ 2×3=6? 7 > 6, invalid.

For [3,5]: 5 ≤ 2×3=6 ✓, valid, length = 2
For [4,5,7]: 7 ≤ 2×4=8 ✓, valid, length = 3
For [5,7,8]: 8 ≤ 2×5=10 ✓, valid, length = 3

Maximum valid length = 3, so removals = 6 - 3 = 3.

Hmm, still not 2. Let me check the note again.

The note says: remove 4th (8) and 6th (7), leaving [4,5,3,3]. Sorted: [3,3,4,5]. Max=5, min=3, 5 ≤ 2×3=6 ✓. This is valid with 4 elements.

So length 4, removals = 6-4 = 2. Correct!

I need to fix the algorithm - I'm checking the wrong windows.

The algorithm should be:
1. Sort the array
2. For each possible starting position (left), find the furthest right position (right) such that arr[right] ≤ 2 × arr[left]
3. Track the maximum window size

```python
n = int(input())
c = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
c.sort()

max_remaining = 1  # At least one element is always valid

# Use two pointers to find the longest valid subsequence
left = 0
for right in range(n):
    # Shrink window from left while condition is violated
    while left <= right and c[right] > 2 * c[left]:
        left += 1
    
    # If we have a valid window from left to right
    if left <= right:
        window_length = right - left + 1
        max_remaining = max(max_remaining, window_length)

# Minimum removals = total - maximum remaining
print(n - max_remaining)
```

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` measurements with values `c_1, c_2, ..., c_n`
2. We need to remove the minimum number of measurements so that the largest remaining value ≤ 2 × smallest remaining value
3. We want to minimize the number of removals (maximize the number of remaining measurements)

## Key Insights

1. If we sort the measurements, we can consider subarrays where the maximum element ≤ 2 × minimum element
2. For any valid subarray, if we take a subarray from index `i` to `j`, then `c[j] ≤ 2 × c[i]`
3. We want to find the longest such subarray to minimize removals
4. The minimum removals = `n - (length of longest valid subarray)`

## Algorithm Steps

1. Sort the measurements in ascending order
2. For each possible starting position, find the longest valid subarray where `c[j] ≤ 2 × c[i]`
3. Track the maximum length of such subarrays
4. Return `n - max_length`

## Implementation Strategy

- Sort the array
- For each starting position `i`, use a two-pointer technique to extend the subarray as far as possible
- For each starting position, find the maximum `j` such that `c[j] ≤ 2 × c[i]`
- Keep track of the maximum subarray length
- Answer is `n - max_length`

```python
n = int(input())
c = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
c.sort()

max_length = 1  # At minimum, we can always keep one measurement

# Try each possible starting position
for i in range(n):
    # For each starting position, extend as far as possible
    j = i
    while j < n and c[j] <= 2 * c[i]:
        j += 1
    # j is now the first position where c[j] > 2 * c[i]
    # So the valid subarray is from i to j-1
    length = j - i
    max_length = max(max_length, length)

# Minimum removals = total measurements - maximum kept measurements
result = n - max_length
print(result)
```

## Verification with Examples

**Example 1**: `[4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7]`
- Sorted: `[3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]`
- Starting at 0: 3,3,4,5,7 (8 > 2×3=6) → length 4
- Starting at 1: 3,4,5,7 (8 > 2×3=6) → length 4  
- Starting at 2: 4,5,7 (8 > 2×4=8? 8 ≤ 8) → 8 ≤ 8, so length 4
- Starting at 3: 5,7 (8 > 2×5=10? 8 ≤ 10) → length 2
- Starting at 4: 7 (8 > 2×7=14? 8 ≤ 14) → length 1
- Starting at 5: 8 → length 1
- Max length = 4, removals = 6 - 4 = 2 ✓

**Example 2**: `[4, 3, 2, 4]`
- Sorted: `[2, 3, 4, 4]`
- Starting at 0: 2,3,4 (4 ≤ 2×2=4) → length 4
- All starting positions give valid subarrays
- Max length = 4, removals = 4 - 4 = 0 ✓

The solution works correctly!

    # So the valid subarray is from i to j-1

4. Track the maximum length of such subarrays

## Step-by-step Solution

Let me trace through the examples:

**Example 1:** `[4, 5, 3, 8, 3, 7]`
- After sorting: `[3, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8]`
- Check all possible subarrays:
  - `[3,3]`: 3 ≤ 2×3 ✓, length = 2
  - `[3,4]`: 4 ≤ 2×3 ✓, length = 2  
  - `[3,5]`: 5 ≤ 2×3 ✓, length = 2
  - `[3,7]`: 7 ≤ 2×3? 7 ≤ 6? ✗
  - `[3,8]`: 8 ≤ 6? ✗
  - `[4,5]`: 5 ≤ 8 ✓, length = 2
  - `[4,7]`: 7 ≤ 8 ✓, length = 2
  - `[4,8]`: 8 ≤ 8 ✓, length = 2
  - `[5,7]`: 7 ≤ 10 ✓, length = 2
  - `[5,8]`: 8 ≤ 10 ✓, length = 2
  - `[7,8]`: 8 ≤ 14 ✓, length = 2
- Maximum length = 2 (e.g., `[4,5,7,8]` has length 4: max=8, min=4, 8 ≤ 8 ✓)
- Wait, let me recalculate: `[4,5,7,8]`: max=8, min=4, 8 ≤ 8 ✓ (yes!)
- So maximum length = 4, answer = 6 - 4 = 2 ✓

**Example 2:** `[4, 3, 2, 4]`
- After sorting: `[2, 3, 4, 4]`
- Check: `[2,3,4,4]`: max=4, min=2, 4 ≤ 4 ✓
- Maximum length = 4, answer = 4 - 4 = 0 ✓

## Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
measurements.sort()

max_keep = 1  # At minimum, we can keep one measurement

# Try all possible starting positions
for left in range(n):
    # For each starting position, extend as far as possible
    right = left
    while right < n:
        # Check if current subarray [left, right] satisfies condition
        if measurements[right] <= 2 * measurements[left]:
            right += 1
        else:
            break
    
    # The valid length is (right - left)
    length = right - left
    max_keep = max(max_keep, length)

# Minimum removals = total - maximum kept
print(n - max_keep)
```

Wait, I need to reconsider this. In the above code, when I increment `right`, I'm not checking the condition correctly. Let me fix it:

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
measurements.sort()

max_keep = 1  # At minimum, we can keep one measurement

# Try all possible starting positions
for left in range(n):
    right = left
    # Extend right as far as possible while condition holds
    while right < n:
        if measurements[right] <= 2 * measurements[left]:
            right += 1
        else:
            break
    
    # The valid length is (right - left)
    length = right - left
    max_keep = max(max_keep, length)

# Minimum removals = total - maximum kept
print(n - max_keep)
```

Actually, let me trace this again. When `right` reaches the end of the loop, `right` is one past the last valid index, so the length is `right - left`. This is correct.

For example: if valid indices are [left, left+1, left+2], then right becomes left+3, and length = (left+3) - left = 3.

This looks correct. Let me verify with the examples:

Example 1: [3,3,4,5,7,8]
- left=0: check 3≤2×3 ✓, 3≤6 ✓, 4≤6 ✓, 5≤6 ✓, 7≤6 ✗ → right=5, length=5-0=5
- left=1: check 3≤6 ✓, 4≤6 ✓, 5≤6 ✓, 7≤6 ✗ → right=5, length=4
- ... and so on
- Maximum length should be 5? But wait, [3,3,4,5,7,8] has max=8, min=3, 8≤6? No.

I see the mistake. The condition is `measurements[right] <= 2 * measurements[left]`, but when right=5 (value 8), for left=0: 8 ≤ 2×3 = 6? No. 

So at left=0: right starts at 0, then 1: value 3 ≤ 6 ✓, right=1
then 2: value 4 ≤ 6 ✓, right=2  
then 3: value 5 ≤ 6 ✓, right=3
then 4: value 7 ≤ 6 ✗ → break
So right=4, length=4-0=4

For left=1: value 3 ≤ 6 ✓, right=1
then 2: 4 ≤ 6 ✓, right=2  
then 3: 5 ≤ 6 ✓, right=3
then 4: 7 ≤ 6 ✗ → break
length=3

For left=2: value 4 ≤ 8 ✓, right=2
then 3: 5 ≤ 8 ✓, right=3
then 4: 7 ≤ 8 ✓, right=4
then 5: 8 ≤ 8 ✓, right=5
then 6: out of bounds → break
length=5-2=3

For left=3: value 5 ≤ 10 ✓, right=3
then 4: 7 ≤ 10 ✓, right=4
then 5: 8 ≤ 10 ✓, right=5
length=5-3=2

For left=4: value 7 ≤ 14 ✓, right=4
then 5: 8 ≤ 14 ✓, right=5
length=1

For left=5: length=1

Maximum length = 4, so answer = 6-4 = 2 ✓

This matches! The algorithm is correct.

```python
n = int(input())
measurements = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Sort the measurements
measurements.sort()

max_keep = 1  # At minimum, we can keep one measurement

# Try all possible starting positions
for left in range(n):
    right = left
    # Extend right as far as possible while condition holds
    while right < n:
        if measurements[right] <= 2 * measurements[left]:
            right += 1
        else:
            break
    
    # The valid length is (right - left)
    length = right - left
    max_keep = max(max_keep, length)

# Minimum removals = total - maximum kept
print(n - max_keep)
```
